Update 2026-07-20: Sparse Delta Memory (formerly Idea A) moved to Recent Completions as CLOSED-NEGATIVE — GDN-2 beat SDM decisively at 30M composition (val_ppl 72.26 vs 79.66, per docs/HANDOFF_2026_07_15.md) and the follow-up F1 Muon-gate-matrix-fix SDM variant also failed (val_ppl 83.77); remaining architectural ideas renumbered A–F. 500M v3 IS LAUNCHED. torch.compile fixed and shipped (~3x, see reference_torch_compile_delta.md Update 2026-07-17: a real lif_parallel miscompile bug found+fixed, reduce-overhead crashed on an open upstream PyTorch bug so shipped compile_mode: default instead, MQAR gets its own separate compiled wrapper). block_size raised 512→640 via a real VRAM probe at true 500M shape (largest fitting ≤90% of the 5090's 32GB), confirmed via a 30M screen — a genuine quality lever (more context for the memory pathway), not just speed. mqar_cadence=2 (half-frequency MQAR) was tried and REJECTED: val_ppl looked great (37.51 vs 58.09, 2-seed confirmed) but recall_eval showed a real recall regression at high N (N=32 56%→36%, N=64 34%→17%) — general capability improving while recall degrades is the exact failure mode this project's memory-first design watches for; left at default (every step). The sparsity_lambda A/B (1.5 vs 3.0 vs default 0.5) also came back negative — both higher-lambda arms cut memory-Δ substantially (0.5: 156 → 1.5: 105 → 3.0: 65) for a lower spike rate that wasn't worth the memory-pathway cost; kept default 0.5. Pre-launch audit (Fable-on-High) found and fixed 5 real issues: leftover smoke checkpoint (deleted), engram_factual_eval.py's build() drift (also missing gdn2_enabled, worse than first suspected — fixed, re-confirmed Engram h4 helps), an EngramV2 prime-count assert undercount, unbounded brain-snapshot growth (snapshot_brain: false), and crash-resume not advancing the data RNG/curriculum counter. See neuro_500m_v3_launched.md memory and journey §45 for full detail. Older completions and rejected candidates live in to-do-archive.html.

Update 2026-09-07 (LR sweep result) — higher muon_lr helps val_ppl but HURTS recall; 0.005 already right for recall. muon_lr {0.005/0.01/0.02/0.04} one-variable on [4,32]+w0.5. val_ppl has an interior optimum at 0.02 (64.3 vs 73.0 baseline) but recall is best at the current 0.005 and declines monotonically as LR rises (single-pres N16 95→62→53→12; N32 52→53→25→8) — a clean recall-vs-ppl tradeoff; 0.04 overshoots both. Confirms arXiv:2508.19029 (recall IS strongly LR-sensitive, and its optimum ≠ ppl's) but the free lever points the wrong way for us. De-risk (not a change): flagship muon_lr=0.005 sits at/near the RECALL optimum — don't raise it (matches the earlier W3@7e-4 recall-rank rejection). Open cheap thread: recall improved all the way down to 0.005, so the optimum may be BELOW it (a 0.0025/0.00375 probe could squeeze recall at some ppl cost). Memory neuro_muon_lr_recall_tradeoff_2026_09_07; results logs/lr_sweep_recall_{SINGLE,SPLIT}.log. 5080 now free — released to Glenn.

Update 2026-09-07 (lever F built) — head-specific weight decay added (config-gated) + screens staged; NOT launched (5080 reserved for Glenn after the LR sweep). Targets the flagship's recurring killer — runaway LM-head norm (z_loss 1e-3 was null). Routing insight: the LM head already sits in AdamW's decay group, but decoupled decay at the global wd (~lr·wd/step) is too weak to hold it; mtp_proj is in Muon (its own wd). New head_weight_decay key (muon.build_muon_adamw + train_gpu._build_optimizers) splits the LM head into its own AdamW group at a higher wd. Verified CPU-only: default (None) is byte-identical grouping (flagship resume-safe; both modules import clean); when set, head.weight (16.78M) isolates at the chosen wd, head.bias stays undecayed, Muon untouched, params conserved. NOT resume-compat when ON (adds a param group) → fresh runs only. Staged (single-seed, wd0.1 flagship regime in all arms): screen_headwd_{base,0p5,1p0,2p0}_s0_30m. 30M tests SAFETY/neutrality (must not hurt val_ppl/recall) + head-[wnorm] trajectory; can't reproduce the late divergence itself — real test is folding a safe winner into v3. Do NOT graft into running v2 (healthy, head-norm receding; and param-group layout change breaks resume). Plan: test F when 5080 free → fold winner to v3 → then config levers A→E in order on Glenn's signal.

Update 2026-09-06 — 2-seed flagship-v3 MQAR confirm: adopt the RANGE, HOLD the weight. Baseline (fixed-16 + w0.5) vs proposed ([4,32] + w1.0), 2 seeds each, 30M. Recall win is ROBUST: proposed beats baseline at every N in both seeds (N32 70/53 vs 34/33, N48 44/23 vs 11/9). But val_ppl is seed-fragile for the weight bump: baseline 73.4/73.2 (tight) vs proposed 68.9 / 83.4 — seed 1 converged much slower (monotonic 145→83, not a divergence). Attribution: the RANGE change drives ~2/3 of the recall gain with zero ppl cost; the WEIGHT change adds the rest but carries the ppl variance. Refined rec: flagship-v3 should adopt MQAR range [4,32] (robust, safe, free) and HOLD weight 1.0 pending a 90M/more-tokens check (the ppl wobble may be 30M undertraining variance that washes out, or a real cost — unknown). The 2-seed screen caught a seed-fragility the single-seed double-win hid. Memory neuro_mqar_range_30m_2026_09_03.

Update 2026-09-03 (overnight) — MQAR curriculum curve mapped + recall-ceiling plan drafted. Attacked the one real deficit (high-N recall). Diagnostic: GDN-2 already runs n_heads=1 = full dense state, yet collapses at N≥32 — so it’s an INTERFERENCE limit, not a state-size limit. MQAR N_max curve @30M {16,32,48,64}: the recall horizon tracks the trained range, but peak recall is MAXIMIZED at [4,32] and DEGRADES as you widen (wider → worse recall, monotonically BETTER val_ppl 75.8→63.0 — a recall-vs-ppl tradeoff). Free flagship-v3 win: switch MQAR from fixed-16 to range ~[4,32] — fixed-16 is very likely why the flagship collapses at N=32 (8%) where 30M [4,32] holds 52%; confirm @90M. Design doc docs/recall_ceiling_design_2026_09.md: ranked ceiling-raisers — free (MQAR-range + split-replay), cheap-code (StateX post-train state expansion), big-bet (DeltaProduct arXiv:2502.10297, gated behind a kernel de-risk spike per the log-linear lesson). No code written (held for review; touches the shared trainer/kernel). Config-only tests only; flagship untouched (val_ppl 32.8, descending). Details: memory neuro_mqar_range_30m_2026_09_03.

Update 2026-09-03 — arch head-to-head (lm_eval + recall) DONE on the 5080; multi-axis picture now complete. Ran the last open item from the transformer-baseline comparison — downstream benchmarks + zero-shot recall on both 363M bases. Downstream (lm_eval, base/un-tuned): the transformer is competitive-to-BETTER despite its worse val_ppl — arc_easy 44.6 vs 38.9, hellaswag 31.4 vs 29.8, both at chance on arc_challenge/mmlu. In-context recall — a crossover: spiking wins small-N (N≤16: 100/98/94 vs 81/73/58) but its single fixed state COLLAPSES at N≥32 (8/6/2%) while the transformer degrades gracefully (55/42/31%); split-replay recovers spiking to 56/27/16 (~ties at N=32, trails at 64/128). Directly confirms the fixed-state recall ceiling that log-linear tried and failed to lift. Net: “our arch wins” holds on val_ppl (~34%) + the energy / persistent-portable-state / constant-seq-cost differentiators, but NOT on downstream MC accuracy or high-N recall. Honest caveats: un-tuned bases near chance except arc_easy; spiking got fewer tokens (4.87 vs 5.50B). Preliminary (do not over-read at 9%): the transformer is currently BEHIND the spiking flagship at matched tokens (~47 vs the flagship's ~39-40 at ~500M tok) — early signal our arch leads, but LR schedules differ and val_ppl isn't the whole story (no memory-Δ/recall for the transformer); the real verdict is at completion. Log-linear attention scoped → docs/loglinear_attention_design.md. Recommendation: de-risk with a 1-2 day kernel spike BEFORE any flagship commit; do flagship-v2 (Tier-1) first regardless. It's the only on-target attack on the proven recall ceiling and composes with GDN-2, BUT it's a heavy, numerically-dangerous kernel project: (1) the hierarchical combine multiplies more decay factors across O(log n) levels — exactly the overflow class _gdn2_dense_chunk already had to hand-factorize around; (2) VRAM: ~9 simultaneous states × 8 delta layers ≈ +8.5 GB fp32 at B=18 → OOMs the 32 GB card (mitigable via bf16/coarser hierarchy/subset-of-layers, each a knob to tune). Two-stage gate: Stage A = single-layer kernel parity+NaN+VRAM spike at 30M (5080-eligible); Stage B = 30M→90M 2-seed screen, pre-registered win = recall_eval --replay split up at N=32 AND N=64 beyond seed noise, val_ppl within noise, throughput regression <25%. HOLA (arXiv:2607.02303) is the fallback if the kernel proves intractable, but it breaks the portable-fixed-state differentiator so it's off-mission. Memory neuro_final_norm_attention_stack, plan docs/improvement_plan_2026_08.md.

Winner sets the 363M baseline's LR (and informs flagship-v2). Methodology note: this makes it "each arch at its community-tuned LR" (the blog's approach), not strict same-LR — flag if you'd prefer strict matching. Update 2026-08-25 — FLAGSHIP DIVERGENCE ROOT-CAUSED: runaway LM-head norm growth. Checkpoint forensics (temp/divergence_ckpt_diff.py, temp/divergence_head_breakdown.py) found head.weight L2 norm flat at ~149–150 from step 112k–124k then going superlinear (+1.4 → +10.5 → +33.6 per 4k steps, +29.2% total 124k→132k), with mtp_proj.0/2 doing the same (+27.8%/+18.9%). Every block moved 0.66–0.77× (less, as LR decays — normal) and embed.weight decayed smoothly −2.7% throughout. Total ‖W‖ bottomed at 124k, the exact val_ppl minimum. All weights finite, max|w| 17.2→17.6 — a bifurcation localized to the output heads, not a numerical blowup. Mechanism: bigger head → bigger logits → sharper softmax → overconfidence; z_loss_weight: 0.0001 is the guard and was too weak. Causal order resolved: head runaway (~126k) → val_ppl rises (128k) → spike rate rises (130k), so the sparsity-boundary lead was a downstream symptom. ELIMINATED: resume/data-replay (all 4 resumes were the Aug-12 20:05–20:26 restart episode, none near 130k), data wrap (train.bin ~29B tok, run used 4.79B), LR magnitude (diverged AT the cosine minimum), numerics. Next-run fixes (ranked): (1) head-norm + logit-RMS telemetry per eval — this was invisible live, found only by post-hoc ckpt diffing; (2) best-ckpt tracking / early-stop on val_ppl (would have saved ~11k steps, ~18h); (3) stronger z_loss (1e-3) and verify it covers MTP logits, not just the main head; (4) optional logit soft-cap. Memory neuro_flagship_divergence_root_cause.

Phase 3 (future work, post-500M-v3): recursion

Recursion/recurrent-depth is OFF the 500M v3 critical path. Two sub-questions, both already answered for the cheap version:

  • Uniform/fixed-depth looping (Huginn-style, no router): CLOSED NEGATIVE, 2-seed confirmed. Fair matched-token control (depth-1 74.55/73.35, seeds 0/1) beats plain depth-2 (77.35/78.52) and depth-2+deep-supervision (79.49/76.94). See neuro_adaptive_compute.md for the full multi-round investigation (includes two rounds of ruling out eval-noise and token-budget confounds before reaching this verdict). Do not re-test this form.
  • Per-token adaptive router (MoR proper, the paper's actual claim): UNRESOLVED, needs real engineering before it can be tested fairly. Stage 2 (RecurrentDepthRouter in src/adaptive_compute.py) only ever implemented the dense/masked variant — every token gets the full extra pass computed regardless of the router's decision, so there is currently zero FLOP saving from routing. The sparse-gather Phase 2 (only compute the extra pass for tokens the router actually keeps) was scoped from day one but never built. Tonight's screen_mor_200m_2b ran at ~2295 tok/s (7.7 days for 2B tokens) because of this gap, and was killed by Glenn as impractical. The paper's own ablations need >=135M params AND ~0.9-2.3B tokens to beat vanilla even with a working sparse implementation. Next step if revisited: build the sparse-gather Phase 2 first, then re-run a MoR screen at a token budget the paper says is meaningful — not before 500M v3 ships.

DeepSeek/Qwen/Kimi) reshaped the post-training plan: on-policy distillation is the 2026 workhorse for small models, reasoning is capacity-limited <1B (MobileLLM-R1), and our DPO collapse is textbook likelihood displacement (see neuro_posttrain_v3_prep_2026_09_13). KEY FINDING: the tokenizer is Llama-3 (src/tokenizer.py; train.bin projected to vocab 32768 via data/vocab_map_32768.npy), so a Llama-family teacher IS usable for v3 — the logit-projection KD infra already exists + is verified (src/kd.py, top-128 128k→32k), teachers cached locally (Llama-3.1-8B / 3.2-1B). Ran the ORPO probe on the 5080: ORPO holds coherence to step 675 (11.1M tok) where DPO word-souped by step 100 (0.8M) — 12×+ margin, correct answers at steps 200/300/400/600 (step-500 a transient greedy wobble). It OOM'd on a WDDM VRAM spill (batch 4 = 8 seqs, torch reserved 20.1GB > 16GB 5080), NOT a quality failure; the auto recall_eval (written at --B 128, too big for 16GB) was killed, so the memory-pathway half is UNMEASURED (orpo_probe_363m/step_0000500.pt saved for it). 5080 finding: ~300 tok/s eager 363M + VRAM-tight → the A curriculum chain is infeasible on it in one night; needs the 5090 or a multi-day window, and any 5080 post-training needs batch_size≤4 + recall --B≤32. Update 2026-09-20 (post-training stack EXTENDED to 3 stages and BUILT; data generating on the 5080). A research sweep produced a shortlist; Glenn picked #1 on-policy distillation, #4 tool calling, #5 why-narrated alignment (then #2 abstention, #3 confidence head, #6 energy/verifier head if time). Built + unit-tested today: (1) src/train_opd.py — on-policy distillation, the stage Qwen3/MiMo/GLM-5/MiniCPM5 all converged on. The student samples its own rollouts and a frozen Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct teacher scores every token (support-restricted reverse KL + an out-of-support mass penalty). The teacher MUST use the Llama-3 tokenizer: our 32768 vocab is a LUT projection of Llama-3's 128256 and the LUT is injective on nonzero values, so inv[student_id] aligns the two sequences position-for-position. Rollouts use full-prefix re-forward, NOT incremental initial_state decoding, because hybrid_attn.py keeps no KV cache. (2) Tool calling — a locked TOOL {json} / ANSWER <text> protocol (src/tool_data.py; 9-tool registry in training_data/posttrain/tools.json) plus a 501-node DFA for grammar-constrained decoding (src/grammar.py) that allows only 5 of 32768 tokens at the first response position and rejects a wrong tool name, wrong argument order, a number passed as a string, or any tool absent from the offered list. (3) Data generator — scripts/gen_teacher_data.py, four resumable targets (tools / why / abstain / coldstart) with progress sidecars, running on the 5080 at ~600 records/min. CRITICAL BUG FOUND + FIXED: load_lut_encode silently DROPS every Llama-3 token outside our 32k vocab (74.5% of Llama-3's vocab maps to id 0), so "calculator" encoded to "" and "kilometers" to "ometers" — it would have corrupted most tool targets invisibly. Fix = src/lut_spell.py re-spells OOV tokens out of in-vocab subwords, opt-in via lut_subword_fallback: true (default off, so existing runs stay byte-identical). Detail: neuro_lut_oov_drop_bug, neuro_posttrain_v3_stack_built_2026_09_20, reference_teacher_gen_5080.

Teacher data generated on the 5080: tools 24,000 / abstain 16,000 / coldstart 16,000 (.done markers); why deferred at 128 — relaunch temp/run_5080_posttrain_gen.sh and it resumes. OPD GPU smoke PASSED on a real 363M student + real 3B teacher (no_sup 0.000 = the teacher's top-16 always lands inside our 32k vocab), 13.7/14.8 GB → OPD is 5090-only. First MFU measurement this project has taken: 27.3% (scripts/mfu_report.py) — the three GDN-2 gates are ~25% of all FLOPs while ALL attention is 1.8%, so FlashAttention-class work is irrelevant here. gate_rank low-rank gates implemented (GLA rank-16 precedent): −27% params, −23% FLOPs at r=32, default 0 = byte-identical, quality unscreened. Update 2026-09-21 — gate_rank SCREENED, the one architectural throughput lever is a WIN. 2-seed 30M A/B over {0,16,32} (temp/run_5080_gaterank.sh, six configs/screen_gaterank_r*_s*_30m.yaml). Low-rank gates don't just match full-rank — they beat it: val_ppl 2-seed mean r0=72.0 / r16=60.6 / r32=68.8, and split-replay recall (B=32) holds at every N (r16/r32 hit 100% at N=16 where the full-rank control sags to ~92%). The rank-16 bottleneck regularizes gates that otherwise overfit — exactly GLA's finding. DECISION: adopt gate_rank:16 in flagship_v4 (r32 = conservative fallback). Staged into configs/flagship_v4_363m.draft.yaml. Minor: recall_eval.py prints a stale "single presentation" header regardless of --replay (the flag works; 1-line cosmetic fix). Detail: neuro_v4_throughput_mfu_2026_09_20, journey §63.

flagship_v4 throughput levers (queue, in order):

  • 1. gate_rank 30M screen, 2-seed, {0, 16, 32} — DONE 2026-09-21 — A WIN, adopt gate_rank:16. val_ppl 2-seed mean r0=72.0 / r16=60.6 (−16%) / r32=68.8 (−4%): low-rank BEATS full-rank (bottleneck regularizes gates that overfit; matches GLA rank-16). Recall (split-replay B=32) HOLDS — r16/r32 match or beat control at every N (both 100% at N=16 vs r0 ~92%). Params 55.1M→49.0M (−11% at 30M; the −27% is at flagship width). Detail: neuro_v4_throughput_mfu_2026_09_20, logs/gaterank_screen.log.
  • 2. KV cache in src/hybrid_attn.py (step() + growing cache, as its own docstring proposes) — OPD rollouts run at 14–17 tok/s because there is no cache and every generated token re-forwards the whole prefix. Pays off in OPD, tool_eval, abstain_eval, head_eval, self_refine and the TTFT benchmark. Does NOT touch pretrain MFU.
  • 3. FP8 (E4M3) via torchao — 1.25–1.4×, Blackwell-native. The “Blackwell int8 regression” note is about inference int8 and does not apply. Borrow the recipe from IST-DASLab/llmq (61–70% MFU on consumer cards).
  • 4. CUDA graphs — still blocked on the MQAR restructure (neuro_cudagraphs_incompat_2026_08_09); 1.15–1.3× and it attacks the overhead-bound regime directly.
  • 5. Curation, not acquisition — 20B tokens exceeds our 14.53B corpus, but the pools (~79.5B) are just not on disk. Rebalance away from 38% raw DCLM toward FineWeb-Edu (9.68B available, only 3.30B used; its classifier is worth 4–6 MMLU points at fixed compute), then a DoReMi-style proxy sweep on the existing 30M/90M harness to set weights properly. data/tokenized_phi is 66GB of reclaimable dead weight from the Phi-vocab era.
  • 6. Then re-measure and decide: ~31 days on the 5090 vs ~6.3 days / ~$230 on a single H100 spot (all cloud options cluster at $230–270, so the choice is wall-clock not money).

Update 2026-08-02 (QK-norm CONFIRMED, Steps 1-2 done): QK-norm shipped (src/hybrid_attn.py, attention_qk_norm config flag) and screened overnight per the plan above. 30M 3-seed: spread 90%→8% (140.30/149.83/152.06, mean 147.4), beats no-attn 287. 90M 3-seed: spread 62%→11% (54.76/52.52/58.53, mean 55.27). H1 (attention instability) confirmed as the mechanism; AdamW-routing/longer-warmup fallbacks were not needed. Full tables in docs/model_quality_redesign.md §"2026-08-02 — Step 1 + Step 2 CONFIRMED". Update 2026-08-02 (STEP 3 SCOPED + LAUNCHED): Full design in docs/step3_midscale_design.md; memory neuro_step3_midscale_plan.md. Research finding that reshaped it: a systematic hybrid-linear-attention study (arXiv 2507.06457) shows recall — not perplexity — determines the optimal linear:full attention ratio (ppl flat across ratios; recall nearly doubles 24:1→3:1, saturating ~3:1; Qwen3-Next/Qwen3.5 ship 3:1). We run 6:1 and every screen this project has run measured val_ppl only — a structural blind spot, same class as the mqar_cadence rejection. Measured: torch.compile WORKS with HybridAttentionBlock (never tested together before) — 2.3x faster AND lower VRAM, turning Step 3 from ~5 days into ~2; Step-3 shape (d=1152/n=2752/L=8, 267M) hits 17,690 tok/s @ batch 28 / 27.0GB on the 5090; WDDM spill reconfirmed live on the 5080 (batch 32 → 19.0GB on a 16GB card → throughput COLLAPSED 5,803→1,367 tok/s). Confound found: spike_rate is a flat mean over all layers and attention layers emit zero spikes, so 1→2 attention layers mechanically drops reported spike rate ~14% — must normalize by n_spiking/n_total before comparing arms. Plan (~2.6 days): Phase 0 compile-parity gate at 30M (~1.5h, vs known eager 140.30); Phase 1 attention-ratio screen 7:1 vs 3:1, 2 seeds, all on the 5090, judged on recall_eval + memory-Δ, not val_ppl (~8h); Phase 2 Step 3 proper — 3 seeds × 267M × 1.1B tokens (~52h). Note: prior 30M/90M screens all used n_neurons=d, but 500M v3 / the 1B design use n≈2.4d — the screens were never shape-faithful to the target; Step 3 fixes that. 5080 runs the λ=0.5 confirm during Phase 2.

Update 2026-08-03 (Phase 0/1 done, key findings): Phase 0 PASSED (compile+attention quality-neutral: val_ppl 142.10 vs eager 140.30, +1.28%). λ=0.5 CONFIRMED at 90M 2-seed (val_ppl tied 55.9 vs 55.3, spike 0.204 vs 0.238, tighter seed spread) — adopt for Phase 2. Phase 1 flipped the arm decision DECISIVELY. val_ppl and memory-Δ said the arms were tied/B-slightly-ahead, but recall_eval (--replay split, N=4/16/64) crushed the 3:1 arm: 7:1 hit 100/78/59% and 100/94/47% (seeds 0 & 1); 3:1 collapsed to 31/9/3% (~chance). Confirmed via without-split-replay control (97/89/28% vs 41/8/2%), so it's not a split-replay artifact — 3:1 just failed to learn associative recall at all. Same failure mode as the mqar_cadence=2 rejection: general capability improving while recall regresses. arXiv 2507.06457's "more attention = more recall" measures IN-CONTEXT recall (RULER-style); our --replay split measures PERSISTENT-MEMORY recall, which IS the project's differentiator. Recorded in neuro_phase1_attnratio_finding.md. Corollary: memory-Δ alone is insufficient — 3:1 had HIGHER memory-Δ (+255 vs +231) while having ~zero recall; pair memory-Δ with recall_eval going forward. Phase 2 will use 7:1 attention (position [3] in an 8-layer stack), λ=0.5 with a memory-Δ tripwire on first 3 evals. Running overnight: 3:1 seed-1 for 2-seed confirmation of the disaster; λ=0.5 at the new aspect ratio (d=640/n=1536/L=8 proxy for Phase 2); Phase 2 config prep + 267M throughput/VRAM probe with block_size=640.

Update 2026-08-05 (Step 3 diverged and fixed; stability restored): Step 3 (276M, λ=0.5, lr=5e-4) diverged — val_ppl bottomed 97.93 then climbed, spike ran away against the sparsity penalty, GDN-2 write/decay gates in blocks 1–2 collapsed to floor. Diagnostic arms confirmed hyperparameters only DELAYED the failure. Root cause: Muon had no weight_decay parameter at all — entire Muon group + AdamW group trained undecayed for the whole project; Moonshot arXiv 2502.16982 names this exact failure. Two fixes landed (both default off, back-compat verified): Muon decoupled weight decay (AdamW group split so wd hits only 2D weights, unit-tested exact to 8.6e-9) and gdn2_gate_norm RMSNorm on all three gate pre-activations preserving W_fdecay's slow-decay init via gate_bias_f. [gates sat] telemetry added — erase/decay saturation were unmonitored the whole investigation. Separate silent bug found and fixed: energy report used c.get("n_layer", 2) against configs writing n_layers — every energy report ever printed compared our 4–8 layer model to a 2-layer transformer. Matched-depth recompute: efficiency advantage is ~2× stronger than reported and improves with scale (276M: 1.99× at seq 512, 2.74× at seq 4096). Config-key audit found no other silent-key bugs of this class. WSL VRAM incident: gate-norm's activation memory + batch 28 hit dxgkio_make_resident: -12 ENOMEM at step ~2500 (clean process exits, no traceback). Fix: batch 16 / grad_accum 2 (eff 32) for any run with gdn2_gate_norm: true. Arm B (gn+wd+hot lr, batch 16) is past 88M tokens, val_ppl 63.60 monotonically descending, spike at target 0.151, blk1/2 at_floor 1–3% (arm 3 was 47–66% here). The collapse is fixed. Full detail: journey §49, docs/pre_350m_gotchas.md, memory neuro_step3_divergence_2026_08_04, docs/HANDOFF_2026_08_05.md.

  1. Decay-gate saturation research + A/B queue — CLOSED NULL 2026-08-17, DO NOT BUILD. Phase 0a ran (inference-only f_logit clamp on step_0124000.pt, docs/decay_gate_saturation_design.md §0, log logs/phase0a_clamp_sweep.log): fully desaturating every block (decay_sat_lo 99/35/42/16% → 0%) changed split-replay recall by EXACTLY ZERO (100/100/97/50/22 both arms; identity check passed). Pushing further toward forgetting monotonically destroyed recall (floor −2.0 → N=32 halved, N=64 to 0%). Never-forget is load-bearing, not pathological; recall is interference-limited, not retention-limited (blk0 α 0.998→0.992 cuts 128-token retention ~77%→~36% with zero recall change). All four planned arms push gates the wrong way — cancelled. Surviving value: the gate is W_fdecay NOT W_alpha (the original sketch would have been a silent no-op A/B), and literature says gates→1.0 in deeper layers is the normal pattern so blk6/7/8 drift was likely healthy.     Ordering rationale: PMA first so refines start from a stabler base; refines before SFT so the pretrain-shape knobs (recall, sparsity) settle before instruction-following is trained on top; LoRA replaces full-finetune from step 5 onward because the SFT/DPO/brain-mod stages benefit most (small artifacts, tiny VRAM, swappable skills for the demo).

New Architectural Ideas

Post-500M-v3-launch state (2026-07-20): the delta-rule write-rule slot is now settled — GDN-2 won and is locked into 500M v3 as gdn2_chunked: true (see Recent Completions for SDM CLOSED-NEGATIVE 2026-07-15 and the 500M_v3 launch entries). Remaining ideas here are downstream/post-pretrain (LoRA) or exploratory (curriculum, EBM-lite, PowLU) — none gates the running 500M v3.

  1. GDN-2 — erase/write decoupling in the delta rule (BUILT 2026-07-11, UNBLOCKED 2026-07-12 via grad-ckpt, queued on 5090 follow-on chain) — arXiv:2605.22791, NVLabs. The same paper whose β-collapse diagnosis gave us beta_floor; the floor is a patch, this is the actual fix. Standard gated delta uses a single scalar to control two different things — how much old content to erase (key side) and how much new to commit (value side). GDN-2 splits them into a channel-wise erase gate b_t = σ(W_b x) and channel-wise write gate w_t = σ(W_w x), with channel-wise decay D_t = diag(α_t). State update (their layout): S_t = (I − k_t(b_t⊙k_t)ᵀ)D_t S_{t−1} + k_t(w_t⊙v_t)ᵀ; reduces to KDA when the gates tie to a scalar and to Gated DeltaNet when decay also collapses. Why it fits: targets exactly the multi-key retrieval our MQAR refines couldn't move, and the write gate w_t carries the beta_floor stability lesson forward as a per-channel floor.
    Landed 2026-07-11:
    • src/model.py — gdn2_enabled: false flag; when on, adds W_b, W_w, W_fdecay (Linear(N,d_mem)) and gdn2_a (Param(d_mem)). Sequential memory-loop kernel inside forward_sequence (LIF stays FPT-parallel).
    • src/delta_chunked.py — gdn2_sequential reference oracle (verified: reduces exactly to delta_sequential_gated with tied scalar gates).
    • src/train_gpu.py + scripts/recall_eval.py — config plumbing back-ported.
    • configs/screen_gdn2_osdn_ab_control_30m.yaml + configs/screen_gdn2_ab_treatment_30m.yaml (shared depth-1 recipe, mqar_aug: true, differ only in gdn2_enabled).
    • Verified (CPU): 16/16 checks pass in temp/test_gdn2_osdn.py — oracle reduce-to-baseline, flag-off state_dict identical + forward bit-identical, flag-on trains with finite grads, guards fire (needs delta_rule, mutually exclusive with OSDN, rejects n_heads>1).
    Test / gate: 30M A/B (control + this treatment), val_ppl win > ~1.5 ppl noise floor and memory-Δ/recall_eval lift, then seed1 confirm before baking into the next 500M.
    OOM finding → FIX (2026-07-12 01:00): the sequential memory-loop's autograd tape was ~50 GB at batch=4. Fixed same session via grad-checkpointing: extracted _gdn2_chunk_fwd module-level helper, wrapped in torch.utils.checkpoint(use_reentrant=False) per chunk of 32 timesteps (~16x memory reduction, ~2x compute hit during backward). Verified in temp/test_gdn2_osdn_gradckpt.py (8/8 checks + 16/16 original oracle checks): train-mode-with-ckpt is bit-exact to eval-mode-no-ckpt on both forward and grad-signature, peak VRAM measurably lower. Treatment restored to batch=32/grad_accum=1.
    RUNNING 2026-07-12 09:30: 30M treatment ~70% through, val_ppl already beating the control's final number. Mid-run eval showed an anomalous memory-Δ +313.65 (~3× anything else measured) — investigated: ablation path correctly skips the GDN-2 branch (not a code bug); NVIDIA's own reference init (github.com/NVlabs/GatedDeltaNet-2) differs from ours (no-bias Xavier-uniform gates vs our biased-slow-decay init), but our own plain-gated-delta control had already hit +211 memory-Δ on the same slow-decay bias at seed1 — so +313 reads as real per-channel-gating signal amplifying existing seed variance, not a broken measurement. Run continuing; init-matching A/B banked as a follow-up only if this screen wins.
  2. OSDN — online preconditioned delta rule (BUILT 2026-07-11, UNBLOCKED 2026-07-12 via same grad-ckpt fix, queued on 5090 follow-on chain) — arXiv:2605.13473. Adds a diagonal preconditioner d_t to the delta write: error is read with the plain key, but the rank-1 write goes along the preconditioned key k̃_t = d_t ⊙ k_t. Crucially d_t is not a learned parameter — it is online-adapted per step by a closed-form hypergradient (d_{t+1} = d_t + (ηβ/‖k‖²)(1 − β⟨d_t,k²⟩)k², boxed to [0.5,2.0], η=0.003), so it adds ~zero params and only a cheap per-step vector state. Reported +32% in-context recall at 340M (near our scale). Why it fits: pure recall lever, orthogonal to gating (composes with our existing decay_gate + beta_floor), very small code change. Because d_t depends only on keys, a chunked kernel can later precompute it in one pass.
    Landed 2026-07-11:
    • src/model.py — osdn_enabled: false flag; when on, adds zero learned params (verified) — d_t is online state, reset per forward-sequence window. Sequential memory-loop kernel with in-loop preconditioner update.
    • src/delta_chunked.py — osdn_sequential reference oracle (verified: reduces exactly to delta_sequential_gated with η=0; diverges as expected with η>0).
    • configs/screen_osdn_ab_treatment_30m.yaml (shares the same depth-1 control as GDN-2; differs only in osdn_enabled). Paper-default hyperparameters (osdn_eta: 0.003, osdn_dmin: 0.5, osdn_dmax: 2.0).
    • Same 16/16 verification as GDN-2 above.
    Test / gate: same bar as GDN-2 (val_ppl win + memory-Δ/recall lift, then seed1 confirm). Same grad-ckpt fix applied (_osdn_chunk_fwd helper carries M and the online preconditioner d across chunk boundaries). Both queued sequentially on the 5090 follow-on chain (GDN-2 first, OSDN second, LoRA SFT third). If both clear at 30M seed0+seed1, they compose (test the combination at 90M before the 500M bake).
  3. LoRA/PEFT adapters (BUILT & TESTED 2026-07-10, pre-queued for a free 5080 slot) — low-rank adapters for SFT/DPO/brain-mod stages instead of full finetune: faster training, tiny swappable "skill mods," lower VRAM.
    Landed:
    • src/lora.py — LoRALinear (zero-init B = exact no-op at injection, frozen base), inject_lora (targets delta-rule W_q/W_k/W_v by default; skips degenerate 1-row gate matrices), adapter-only save/load, merge_lora (fold-in for zero-overhead inference / brain-mod bake).
    • src/train_sft.py + src/train_dpo.py — additive wiring behind lora_enabled: false default. When on: adapters trained via a single AdamW (Muon is wrong for low-rank factors), full ckpt for resume + a tiny adapter_step_*.pt swappable artifact. DPO adapts only the policy; the frozen base is the reference.
    • scripts/merge_lora_ckpt.py — bake base + adapter → plain merged ckpt (chains LoRA-SFT → merge → LoRA-DPO → merge → demo).
    • configs/sft_30m_lora.yaml — ready to run.
    • Verified (CPU): injection count, bit-identical no-op@init, adapter-only grads, save/load/merge round-trip against the real SpikingHebbianLM, and the full SFT→merge chain reproduces the adapted output.
    Next: run sft_30m_lora.yaml on a free 5080 slot to validate vs full-finetune SFT. Post-pretrain stage — does NOT gate the 350M or the next 500M.
  4. Curriculum warmup (secondary — data-side, zero arch risk, fills idle 5080 slots) — arXiv:2506.11300. Easy-to-hard data ordering for the first ~10% of tokens, reported sustained ~3.5% gains as a pretrain warmup; difficulty signals = compression ratio / lexical diversity / readability. Orthogonal to every arch change, cheap to screen. Test: 30M A/B on ordering alone, seed1 before adopting.
  5. EBM-lite energy/verifier head (post-pretrain, exploratory) — scalar E(context, candidate) trained contrastively to rerank/reject incoherent continuations; targets hallucination/coherence/robustness. NOT full equilibrium-prop (that would rewrite the whole backprop+Muon+KD pipeline — rejected as impractical).
    Scoping note landed 2026-07-10: docs/ebm_lite_design.md — recommends scope now, don't build yet. Post-pretrain inference-time verifier (like LoRA, does NOT gate the 350M). Attaches like the existing confidence_head; negatives = base self-samples + mismatched contexts; loss = margin/InfoNCE. Best home = learned scorer for self-refine critique and/or MTP spec-decode reranking (reuses existing machinery, becomes a demo capability). Hard gate: commit to a pre-registered win condition (ranking-accuracy AUC or best-of-N lift vs base-logprob baseline) BEFORE any code. Implementation-ready doc (2026-07-10): concrete head, per-problem Bradley–Terry loss, EORM data recipe, reference repo to port (github.com/ericjiang18/EnergyORM, arXiv:2505.14999).
  6. Explore activation alternatives: PowLU (and possibly xSwiGLU) (banked, low priority) — researched 2026-07-10, appropriate for a spare 5080 window after the current gates clear. PowLU (arXiv:2605.25704) targets SwiGLU's quadratic-term training instability directly, most relevant if our spiking/delta-rule setup ever shows a stability issue NorMuon/beta_floor don't fix. xSwiGLU / expanded gating range (arXiv:2405.20768) is a near-zero-cost single-scalar widening of the gate, lowest-risk cheap screen.

Recent Completions (this week)

Recent completions kept here for quick reference. Older completions and the full rejected-candidates list live in to-do-archive.html.

  • SDM (Sparse Delta Memory) A/B — CLOSED-NEGATIVE (2026-07-15). Decided against in the 30M composition head-to-head vs GDN-2: SDM val_ppl 79.66 vs GDN-2 72.26, well past the ~1.5 ppl noise floor. GDN-2 is now the locked memory-rule for 500M v3 (gdn2_chunked: true). The F1 Muon-gate-matrix-fix variant of SDM was subsequently tried and also failed (final val_ppl 83.77) — cleaner β/α telemetry but the mechanism still doesn't beat GDN-2. Do not re-launch. Logs: logs/bake_full_sdm_30m.txt (79.66), logs/logs_sdm_f1_treatment_30m.txt (83.77). See docs/HANDOFF_2026_07_15.md and neuro_gdn2_osdn.md. Item was previously listed as Idea A (top strategic priority) — now removed from New Architectural Ideas.
  • F7 landed & exceeded scope: GDN-2 dense chunked-WY kernel, 500M restart named 500M_v3 (2026-07-15). Investigated the fla.ops.gdn2 Triton kernel for the GDN-2 speedup (F7); found its hard 256 key-headdim cap forces block-diagonal multi-head splitting that COLLAPSES MQAR recall as head-count rises (H=1 dense 91% N=16 accuracy → H=4 70% → H=8 11% → H=16 8%, a cliff after H=4 — confirmed architectural not a fixable kernel-precision bug via same-weights loop-vs-kernel isolation). Rejected the fla kernel; instead derived and built an EXACT chunked-parallel GEMM reformulation of the dense-state recurrence (gdn2_chunked_dense in src/delta_chunked.py, GLA-style two-level anchored gating, all exponentials ≤1). Three real bugs found+fixed via actual training runs (not just synthetic parity): autocast silently downcasting internal fp32 GEMMs to bf16 (NaN by step 100) — hard-disabled autocast inside the fn; a midpoint-shift exp factorization overflowing/zeroing on trained fast-decay channels — rewritten as the two-level anchored form; unit_lower_inv Neumann-doubling numerically blowing up on trained MQAR-correlated keys — swapped to torch.linalg.solve_triangular. Wired behind gdn2_chunked config flag (default true) in model.py/train_gpu.py; the loop stays as fallback, use_fla_kernel path stays available but not recommended. 30M validation CONFIRMED: val_ppl 77.03 vs dense-loop champion's 76.98, memory-Δ +299.70 vs +304.54, MQAR recall 89/38/19% (N=16/32/64) vs champion's 91/38/12% — full recall preserved, at ~23x the champion's training speed (23.2k vs ~1k tok/s). Decision: the 500M restart is named 500M_v3 and its config MUST set gdn2_chunked: true (preserves full dense d_mem=2048 state; eliminates GDN-2's ~2x pretraining tax without the fla kernel's recall collapse). See neuro_gdn2_dense_chunked.md.
  • Code-review pass + SDM phase 2 built & CPU-verified (2026-07-12 late-morning). Deep architecture/roadmap review produced 11 findings; 4 applied this session as safe additive changes alongside running chains. F11: α clamp-boundary hi_frac/lo_frac telemetry (analog silent-failure of decay-gate freeze). F4+F6: SDM design-doc amendments — 500M's fla kernel splits d_mem into 8×256² diagonal blocks (~8× less effective per-layer state than dense 4.19M/layer — strengthens the SDM thesis and the sizing table needs to compare against *effective* state); GDN-2/OSDN reframed as per-slot write-rule variants of SDM, NOT mutually exclusive.   • β-collapse recipe fix SHIPPED as beta_floor: 0.1 (2026-07-10, seed-confirmed at 30M + 90M). Root cause: scalar β = sigmoid(W_β·spk) saturates below -20 → β bit-zero → M never written → memory contributes exactly 0. NVIDIA's Gated DeltaNet-2 (arXiv:2605.22791) diagnosis, discovered independently a week earlier. Was affecting every 30M+ delta-rule screen we'd run. Fix1 (beta_floor=0.1) 30M win: val_ppl 65.87 vs baseline 76.43 (-10.6 ppl), memory-Δ +94.44 vs +2.35 (~40× recovery), β dead_frac = 0% across the whole run. Fix2 (vector_beta) SKIPPED — sequential loop OOMs at any reasonable batch on 16GB, and fix1 was decisive. Landed: β telemetry in train_gpu.evaluate() prints [beta] blk0: μ=... dead=X% after each [eval] and marks COLLAPSED if dead_frac≥95%; beta_floor: 0.1 baked into all queued A/B configs; 90M seed1 validation on the exact fully-collapsed failure mode confirmed the fix holds. Standing rule: every new spiking delta_rule config should include beta_floor: 0.1; default 0.0 recovers the collapse pathology. Full writeup: journey.html §33.

Evaluated & rejected — enhancement candidates (as of 2026-07-11)

Recall-lever research verdicts (2026-07-11), items NOT pursued: Erase-then-Delta (arXiv:2606.26560) duplicates GDN-2 — GDN-2 is better validated, pick it. TTT/TNT test-time-training variants overlap what the delta rule already is. Fast-weight Product Key Memory (arXiv:2601.00671) overlaps Engram v2 + Hebbian M — revisit only if Engram v2 underdelivers at 90M. CoT/reasoning distillation is a post-pretrain SFT-stage item (evidence exists sub-1B, e.g. arXiv:2506.07712), not a pretrain gate — defer to the demo phase. Flow-matching, multi-head attention, and full hyper-connections remain rejected (see the line below).

Evaluated & rejected (pre-2026-07-11): Multi-Head Attention (Neuro is deliberately non-attention; delta_rule already fills the role) · Flow-Matching (image/continuous ODE, no fit for AR discrete-token LM) · BLT byte-latent (ground-up tokenizer replacement, incompatible with Phi-3.5+KD pipeline) · DeepSeek V4 Sparse Attention / CSA / HCA / MLA (all attention + KV-cache optimizations — we have neither) · Qwen3-Next hybrid full-attention layers (violates the non-attention thesis; only pays off at very long context). Already done: PMA (keep_last:8 + pma_average.py); Gated DeltaNet (our delta_rule, now also Qwen3-Next's backbone). Already tried & closed negative: Engram v1 (though DeepSeek V4 now runs it at scale — Engram v2 is our fix). Incremental / not worth a dedicated item: 2026 KD refinements (layer-wise / task-aware distillation) — our Phi-3.5 KD already works.

Eval val_ppl monotonically drops (109→111→83→77→72), router keep_rate climbs 9%→43%, memory-Δ +17→+37 — all healthy. Not tied to any block/batch/accum change.
  • Fixed pinned-CPU memory leak in _to_cpu checkpoint staging (was growing WDDM "shared GPU memory" pool each save); confirmed 5080 stable at 0.3GB shared across 5 saves post-fix. 5090's 6.7GB spill is the compiled Phi-3.5 KD teacher, not the checkpoint path — benign, cold reserved blocks, no tok/s impact.
  • 90M MoR seed0 completed: final val_ppl 56.26 @ 400M tok, memory-Δ healthy throughout (+80 to +96, though we now know this was 2-of-4 blocks collapsed and the other 2 carrying it — see β-collapse fix). Energy 1.30-1.74× vs transformer.
  • 90M MoR seed1 (original, pre-β-fix) killed after diagnosing full memory-Δ collapse (+0.00) — root-caused to scalar-β saturation, not a bug in our ablation code. See β-collapse fix.
  • Root-caused and fixed scalar-β write-gate collapse in delta_rule — beta_floor: 0.1, decisive 30M A/B win (val_ppl -10.6, memory-Δ +2.35→+94.44, dead_frac 100%→0%). NVIDIA independently published the same diagnosis (Gated DeltaNet-2, arXiv:2605.22791) a week prior. β telemetry landed in trainer; fix baked into all queued A/B configs. Full writeup: journey.html §33.

Skipped (archived)

  • 30M MoR seed1 confirm (screen_rdepth2_mor_200mtok_seed1_30m.yaml) — 30M is below MoR's ~135M crossover per its own paper, so a clean seed1 wouldn't settle MoR anyway. Not worth the 5080 window when 90M (idea #3) is closer to the crossover and more informative. Config kept in place if we ever want to close the 30M chapter rigorously.

LIF FPT NEVER CONVERGES + exact LIF kernel (2026-09-27, 5080): profiling the v4 draft showed lif_parallel hits the K=10 cap on EVERY call (mean iters 10.00) → the torch.equal early-exit = 10 wasted host syncs/call. vs the true sequential LIF, K=10 FPT disagrees on 21–31% of spikes (K=3/5 vs K=10: ~41%) → the trained neuron is a "10-iteration FPT" function, not a LIF (docstring claim "K=3-5 converge" is false). Scan-chunk lever (512→64) = NO GAIN (scan is memory-bound, ~1–2ms). BUILT src/lif_exact.py: Triton exact sequential LIF (fwd loop + reverse-scan bwd, same atan surrogate); config lif_exact: true (default false = byte-identical). Test vs float64 ref: 0 spike flips, grad rel err ~1e-6 (temp/test_lif_exact.py). 25–50× faster (B8 T512: N3840 fwd+bwd 1.5 vs 26 ms; N9728 2.0 vs 38 ms). 30M SCREEN = CLOSED-NEG as a drop-in (killed after 2 evals, logs/lifexact_screen.log): val_ppl @1k/2k steps 185.7/159.7 vs control 95.7/79.3 (2× worse, not closing); spike rate 0.051 vs 0.175 → the K=10 FPT neuron fires ~3.5× more than a true LIF, and the sparsity penalty (target 0.05) pins the true LIF at target. Peak reserved VRAM 7.9 vs 13.5GB (30M B32). PARKED follow-up (research, not a lever): rate-matched true LIF (no sparsity penalty / lower vth / cur gain) — only if a faithful-LIF story matters for the pitch. No-sync FPT (drop the torch.equal early exit) = VERIFIED bit-identical (30M eager: losses identical over 125 steps) and +20% tok/s on 30M eager screens (11,058→13,292); NO gain on compiled v4 draft B8 (3,415 vs 3,310 — an earlier 2,528 baseline was GPU-contended, ignore).   74. 10-04 22:55 r25 — CHUNKED ATTENTION + MEMORY ACROSS CHUNKS: BUILT + QUEUED 10-04 23:25 (Neuro 51): flags attn_chunk, mqar_xchunk_frac/mqar_xchunk_batch, nlfact_every (+ src/nl_fact_aux.py), all default off (CPU parity + GPU smokes: temp/smoke_r25_cpu.py, temp/smoke_r25_gpu.py). Review + ideas: docs/memory_beyond_window_2026_10_05.md. Baseline r21i with forced chunks: memory Δ +0.15 on chunk 2, cross-chunk haystack and NL facts at chance, in-window NL facts at 200 filler tokens only 28%. attention only inside fixed, ALIGNED chunks (C=512, every layer the same boundaries; train T=1024-2048); the H0 memory runs over the whole sequence with FULL gradient (no TBPTT detach). Why it should work where SWA (r21c/g) and r24a failed: with a sliding window, L×W multi-hop relay crosses any distance; with aligned chunks NO attention layer can cross a boundary, so the memory is the only route to the previous chunk at every depth. Every token keeps exactly the attention context it has at T=512, so the expected ppl cost is ~0 and chunk-2 tokens get memory context for free. Precedents: Block-Recurrent Transformer, Block-State Transformer, RMT/ARMT (BPTT across segments), Llama-4 chunked attention. Also: cross-chunk gap MQAR (kv in chunk A, query in chunk B) so the memory is trained on beyond-window recall (the demo task). Evals: per-chunk ppl mem on/off, cross-chunk haystack, NL fact recall. Weakness to test next: H0 keys are token-level (induction store) → paraphrased NL facts need contextual keys (memory at mid-depth / multi-reader).

  133. RATE-MATCHED EXACT NEURON research (Glenn 2026-09-27). Why FPT never converges here: FPT paper (2506.12087, ICML25) proves contraction only for V_th·L·λ/(1−λ)<1 — they use λ=0.25 and a SIGMOID in the iterations; we use β=0.9 (λ/(1−λ)=9) + hard Heaviside → non-contractive/chaotic. Prior art for exact+parallel: PSN (Fang, NeurIPS23: drop reset → exact parallel, beats LIF), SpikingBrain (2509.05276: adaptive threshold V_th=mean|x|/k, soft reset, integer spikes in training), SPikE-SSM (decoupled reset, learnable thresholds). Diagnostic (temp/diag_neuron_swap.py, trained 30M FPT model, neuron swapped at inference, no retrain): FPT 60.4 ppl @rate .19; all exact variants 190–525 ppl (agreement w/ FPT 86–93%) → co-adapted, must train from scratch. Hypothesis: sparsity penalty (λ0.5, target 0.05) is ineffective on FPT (stays ~0.18) but pins the exact LIF at 0.05 → info-starved. SCREEN RUNNING (temp/run_5080_lifx.sh, logs/lifx_screen.log, auto-kill if eval1>110 or eval2>87.2): exact LIF γ=1 and no-reset PSN γ=0 (new lif_reset key), both sparsity_target 0.18. RESULT exact LIF γ=1 @ target 0.18: eval1 183.2 (rate 0.116) vs control 95.7 → KILLED; vs 185.7 @ target 0.05 → RATE IS NOT THE CAUSE (hypothesis rejected). Queued after γ=0: cheaper-FPT arms fpt_K=3 and fpt_K=1 (config-only; K=10 isn’t converged anyway, so small K is just another learnable neuron at ~3–10× fewer scans). RESULTS (eval1 @1k steps; control FPT K=10 = 95.7): exact no-reset γ=0 @t0.18 195.4 (rate .181 matched!); FPT K=3 235.9 (rate .058); FPT K=1 258.1 (.047) — all auto-killed. Rejected hypotheses: spike rate (matched, still 2×); memoryless-FPT (FPT keeps ~64-token memory, agrees only 80–89% w/ per-token threshold); surrogate-gradient strength (mean g′ equal for FPT10/exact LIF/no-reset, temp/diag_surrogate.py). Found: FPT scan runs in bf16 (β^k weights + membrane rounded) vs kernel fp32 → 5% spike flips on the same input. Parity pattern: odd K ends on an over-spiking guess → heavy resets → low rate & bad. RUNNING/QUEUED: fpt_K=0 (pure-PyTorch bf16 no-reset = kernel check), fpt_K=2, fpt_K=4 (even, 3–5 scans vs 11). Caveat: every recipe knob was tuned with K=10 FPT — an exact neuron may need its own tuning (input scale/LR). NEXT candidates: learnable per-neuron threshold (PLIF-style), SpikingBrain adaptive threshold, γ=0.25–0.5, sigmoid-relaxed FPT iterations.

Likely NOT a bug: induction heads in small transformers typically form only after ~billions of tokens; 100M-token screens are too short to judge transformer recall, while the Hebbian memory binds key→value architecturally (a real sample-efficiency point for spiking). Implications for the 363M fixed baseline: (1) AdamW/transformer-standard LR, not the spiking recipe; (2) report both matched-tokens and matched-compute (tfmr ~6× cheaper/token at 30M); (3) track recall over training to see when/if induction forms. Spiking learns MQAR within the 100M-tok screen → recall sample-efficiency advantage, NOT a transformer bug. Final (600M tok, 1.36h on the 5080): val_ppl 39.7, recall 100/100/100/100/97.

Post-training strategies (6) — screening gated on base hitting 10B (~2026-09-24/25):

  1. On-policy distillation — BUILT + GPU-smoked (src/train_opd.py, configs/opd_v3_363m.yaml). SCREEN pending base. 5090-only.
  2. Calibrated abstention (reward "I don't know") + confidence head + act/confirm/refuse routing — BUILT (16k abstain data, configs/head_conf_v3_363m.yaml, scripts/abstain_eval.py). SCREEN pending base.
  3. Cactus Needle — RESOLVED: = a learned confidence score on the FROZEN v3 model for harness act/confirm/refuse routing + a measurable hallucination number. This IS our confidence head (#2 above / src/heads.py, configs/head_conf_v3_363m.yaml, on-policy labels) — BUILT. Reference recipe to cross-check: github.com/cactus-compute/needle. SCREEN pending base.
  4. Tool-caller, not knower — BUILT (24k tool data + 501-node grammar DFA, src/tool_data.py/src/grammar.py, scripts/tool_eval.py). SCREEN pending base.
  5. Single-shot function-call SFT set — BUILT (this is the tools SFT set from #4). SCREEN pending base.
  6. "why" in ORPO chosen responses + energy/verifier head for best-of-N & refine — BUILT (configs/orpo_v3_363m.yaml, head_energy_v3_363m.yaml); "why" data COMPLETE (6000). SCREEN pending base.
  7. "Sleep" consolidation stage (added 2026-09-25; Behrouz et al., arXiv 2606.03979) — distil what's in the fast-weight (GDN-2/Hebbian) state into the slow weights: teacher = model WITH a doc loaded via split-replay, student = model WITHOUT it, KL on the query tokens (context distillation). Plus "dreaming": the model generates its own rehearsal data, and only samples that improve a held-out metric are kept (ReST-EM). Best-fit 2026 analogue of Glenn's Dream-RSI question. Risks: 426M base is weak (self-generated data amplifies errors); post-pretrain MQAR refine was NULL at 500M. TO-TEST, as a small screen AFTER the verified post-train run, not inserted before it.
  8. On-policy SELF-distillation (OPSD) with privileged info (added 2026-09-25; survey 2605.18141) — the model is its own teacher, conditioned on the reference answer (for us: loaded into the fast state via split-replay). No 3B teacher, ~40–60% less memory than OPD → might fit the 5080. CAUTION 2607.01763: dense self-distillation drifts, forgets, and amplifies formatting artifacts — keep it short, mix in off-policy data. TO-TEST.
  9. Distilled RL (added 2026-09-25; 2607.17247) — teacher-guided RL that reportedly beats both GRPO and OPD. Needs verifiable rewards and a base above chance; ours is at chance on MMLU/ARC-c. PARKED until v4.

[archived 10-22: completed; no follow-up needed here (research; in memory)] 10-12 21:00 (Neuro 59) ONLINE RESEARCH: fixes for silent FFN units (Glenn 19:55; research only, nothing built or launched). What the literature says about the targets: dead units are normal in ReLU-family language models (OPT-66B: over 70% dead in some early layers, Voita 2023; a ReLU transformer loses ~30% of units, layer 0 uses 13% per batch, and masking the unused ones costs nothing, 2407.07848); firing on <1% of units per token costs ~2% loss at 0.5–2B (2603.23198, L1 2e-5). So "fires on ~5% of tokens" is the easy target and "95% alive" is the hard one; the best evidence that fixing dead units buys quality is Aurora (below). Our mechanism, from reading src/muon.py (my analysis, not measured): Muon decay = lr 0.005 × wd 0.1 = 5e-4 per step at peak LR = half-life ~1,400 steps for any row with zero gradient (8× the AdamW group); NorMuon cannot help a row whose gradient is exactly zero (relu2 hard zero). Ideas, all status to-test unless noted: (R1) masked Muon decay: no decay on the w1 rows / w2 columns of units whose update is zero (variant of Cautious Weight Decay, 2510.12402, ICLR 2026, tested with Muon to 1B; their mask is u·x ≥ 0, which still decays a zero-update weight, so ours needs the strict form). Stops the erasing, does not revive; refines idea (c); few lines in Muon._apply_update. (R2) to-verify: ghost × NorMuon: NorMuon divides each w1 row by its own running RMS, so a ghost row gets a FULL-size step whatever the ghost coefficient, and the global renorm then shrinks the live rows' step ~2× (80% of rows join). Prediction: arm G behaves like the harmful broad revival, not like a gentle nudge; if so, fix = keep ghost rows out of the row normalisation or scale them by the coefficient. (R3) U-NorMuon / Aurora (2606.27715, Tilde, code github.com/tilde-research/aurora-release): Muon starves low-leverage rows of TALL matrices (= our FFN w1, 12288×2048) in a self-reinforcing loop; >25% of neurons dead by step 500; 1.1B: loss 2.26 Aurora vs 2.31 Muon vs 2.33 NorMuon; gain grows with FFN expansion (ours 6×). U-NorMuon = stateless row normalisation on tall matrices only (free); Aurora K=2 costs ~24% speed on one GPU. From-scratch evidence only, SwiGLU (no hard zero): for v5, or with R4. (R4) leak in training, exact zero at the end = idea (b) with a template: ProSparse (2402.13516) trains with a soft activation, then a slowly rising L1, then shifts the threshold: 89% sparsity at 7B, 87.9% at 1B, quality kept. (R5) per-unit homeostat with boost: attn_ffn_homeo: unit exists, but theta is clamped at 0 so it can only silence; a per-unit bias that also goes negative (duty-cycle boosting, Numenta 1903.11257; intrinsic plasticity) pushes every unit toward the 5% rate = the target itself. Needs R6 for erased units. RISK to-check: the cloud's layer-wide threshold ramp starts at 60% (step ~136.6k, ~08:30 on 10-13 by my estimate); one threshold per layer silences the weak units first and decay then erases them: watch the alive share across the ramp. (R6) recycle at the start of conversion / SFT (ReDo 2302.12902; Anthropic resampling): dead unit = new input row (toward inputs on high-loss tokens, ~0.2× the mean live norm), output column ZERO (loss unchanged at step 0), optimizer state of those rows reset. temp/n58_revive.py already zeroes the output column, and broad revival was still harmful (72.31); what the literature does differently = small input norm, data-driven rows, optimizer reset, and a small share of units per period (continual backprop, 2306.13812) instead of all at once. Later papers: gains mixed; pick by gradient, not activation (2505.24061). (R7) AuxK for the conversion (OpenAI 2406.04093: removes almost all dead latents, ~10% cost): the top-k dead units get an extra loss to predict what the FFN still misses against the teacher. My adaptation to a language model, unproven. (R8) transcoder route, parked (2406.11944): replace each FFN after training by a wide TopK layer trained layer by layer (cheap on the 5090), then a short KD pass; reaches both targets by construction, costs some ppl, biggest build, good for the interpretability story. (R9) prune instead of revive: removing never-firing units is exactly function-preserving: same ppl from ~0.65B parameters, "share in use" ~100% by shrinking the model; to-decide with Glenn (changes the 1.1B headline). (R10) v5 note: 6× FFN under Muon is where neuron death is worst; either R3 or a narrower FFN. Closed by the literature: plain shrink-and-perturb / L2 toward init (continual-learning tools, no LM-scale evidence).
